<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

~~~~
# Smart Campaign Composer

Marketers describe a campaign in natural language. Claude proposes an
audience segment and SMS/email drafts. A human reviews, edits, and approves,
and approved messages go only to fake users through a local mock provider.
**No real SMS or email is ever sent.**

> **Status: Milestone 3 (AI campaign generation).** The stack starts with
> ~5000 seeded fake users. A JSON segment rule can be validated and sized
> per channel, and a natural-language brief (Turkish or English) becomes a
> validated segment proposal with counts and validated SMS/email drafts,
> using Claude or a deterministic mock. Campaign persistence, approval,
> delivery, and the dashboard are not implemented yet.

## Requirements

- Docker with Docker Compose v2 (required for the stack **and** for
  `make test`, which uses testcontainers)
- Go ≥ 1.21 (the pinned go1.27.3 toolchain is downloaded automatically)
- Node.js ≥ 24 and npm (frontend tests and lint)
- GNU Make

`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is **optional**. Without it the API uses a
deterministic mock LLM, and every test runs without it.

## Quick start

```bash
cp .env.example .env        # placeholders only; adjust if you like
docker compose up --build   # or: make up (detached)
```

| Service       | URL / address                         | Notes                               |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| Frontend      | http://localhost:5173                 | nginx; proxies `/api/*` to the API  |
| API           | http://localhost:8080/healthz, `/readyz` |                                  |
| Mock provider | http://localhost:9090/healthz         | send endpoints come in Milestone 5  |
| Worker        | internal only (`:8081/healthz`, `/readyz`) |                                |
| Postgres, Kafka | internal Compose network only       |                                     |

All published ports bind to `127.0.0.1`. Check status with `docker compose ps`.
Every long-running service reports `healthy`, and the one-shot `migrate` and
`seed` services exit with code 0 (the API starts only after both succeed).

## Seed data
(`http://localhost:5173/api/v1/...`).

## AI generation

Two temporary endpoints turn a brief into proposals. The next milestone
replaces them with campaign endpoints and streaming:

- `POST /api/v1/ai/segment` `{"brief": "..."}` → validated segment rule,
  per-channel counts (same consent-aware counting as the preview), criteria
  the rules cannot express, and a short explanation.
- `POST /api/v1/ai/drafts` `{"brief": "..."}` → validated SMS and email
  drafts plus their worst-case lengths.

Example (mock mode, seeded data; output as observed):

```bash
BRIEF='{"brief":"Son 30 günde sepetine ürün ekleyip alışveriş yapmayan İstanbul'"'"'daki kullanıcılara %30 indirim hatırlatması gönder. Samimi ama kısa olsun."}'
curl -s -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "$BRIEF" localhost:8080/api/v1/ai/segment
# {"rule":{"version":1,"conditions":[{"field":"city","op":"eq","value":"İstanbul"},
#   {"field":"added_to_cart_within_days","op":"eq","value":30},
#   {"field":"no_purchase_within_days","op":"eq","value":30}]},
#  "counts":{"total":389,"sms":262,"email":246},"unsupported_criteria":[],
#  "explanation":"Users matching: city İstanbul; added to cart within 30 days; no purchase within 30 days."}
curl -s -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "$BRIEF" localhost:8080/api/v1/ai/drafts
# {"drafts":{"sms":{"body":"Merhaba {{first_name}}, sepetindeki ürünler seni bekliyor! %30 indirimle bugün tamamla."},
#   "email":{"subject":"{{first_name}}, sepetin seni bekliyor - %30 indirim","body":"Merhaba {{first_name}},\n\n..."}},
#  "worst_case_lengths":{"sms_body":103,"email_subject":67,"email_body":181}}
```

**Mock vs real mode.** With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` empty (or
`LLM_PROVIDER=mock`), a keyword-based mock answers. It recognizes the
example brief's city, cart, and no-purchase phrases, a few others
(Turkish and English), and
… [truncated 5995 chars]
~~~~

</details>

s=s.replace('''# data: {"attempt":1,"field":"sms_body","text":"Merhaba {{first_name}}, s"}''','''# data: {"attempt":1,"field":"sms_body","text":"Merhaba "}''')
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

sed -n 398,440p DECISIONS.md

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

~~~~
### D29. Error taxonomy

`campaign.Error` carries a stable code, a client-safe message, and the
internal cause for logs. Codes and statuses: `invalid_brief` 400,
`unsupported_brief` 422 (with `unsupported_criteria`), `refused` 422,
`invalid_output` 502, `upstream_unavailable` 503, `timeout` 504.
Malformed requests are `invalid_request` (400/415). A caller cancellation
is returned as `context.Canceled`, not a generation error, and is
logged/recorded as 499. A caller deadline maps to `timeout`. Responses
never include model text, SDK errors, or validator output beyond the
codes above.

### D30. Provider selection and the mock

`LLM_PROVIDER` is empty by default: `claude` when a key is set, otherwise
`mock`. `LLM_PROVIDER=mock` keeps the mock even with a key, and
`LLM_PROVIDER=claude` without a key fails at startup instead of silently
using the mock. The mock is deterministic (same brief ⇒ byte-identical
output, streamed in 24-character chunks) and produces output that passes
the real validators. Failure modes (malformed JSON, unknown fields,
unsupported field, overlong SMS, unknown variable, refusal, provider error,
truncation, hang) can only be injected through the Go constructor's script,
so no brief can trigger them.

### D31. New dependency: `anthropic-sdk-go`

The official Go SDK (v1.84.0) provides typed requests, structured-output
parameters, SSE stream accumulation, retries with `retry-after`, and typed
API errors. Hand-written HTTP would duplicate all of that. Adapter tests
point the SDK at an `httptest` server via `option.WithBaseURL`. They cover
success, 429, 5xx/529 with retries, 400 without retry, malformed bodies,
the `refusal` and `max_tokens` stop reasons, and streamed deltas. No test
calls the real API.
~~~~

</details>

**🔧 Bash** — Add M4 decisions and update CLAUDE.md layout

~~~~
cat >> DECISIONS.md <<'EOF'

## Milestone 4 — Campaign approval and content

### D32. Campaign state machine in the domain

`internal/campaign` owns the statuses `draft → audience_ready →
drafts_ready → approved` and a transition table (`CanTransition`).
`drafts_ready → drafts_ready` is a regeneration. Content and channel edits
are allowed only in `drafts_ready`. Approved campaigns are immutable;
Milestone 5 adds sending states after `approved`. Illegal actions return
typed errors (`invalid_state`, 409). The database repeats the status list
in a CHECK constraint and requires `approved_by` and `approved_at`
exactly when the status is `approved`, so a half-approved row cannot exist
even through a bug.

Creation runs segment extraction first and inserts the campaign directly
in `audience_ready`, with the `created` and `audience_proposed` events in
the same transaction. A failed or unsupported proposal stores nothing, so
the list never fills with dead campaigns. `draft` exists in the state
machine and the audit trail, not as a resting state.

### D33. Approval bound to version and content hash

`content_hash` is the SHA-256 of a canonical encoding: a fixed-order JSON
struct with a format version, the normalized segment rule, the channels in
canonical order, and the three message fields. The brief, counts, version,
and status are not part of it. Only the backend computes it.

Every write goes through `Store.Update(id, fn)`. It takes a row lock
(`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`), runs the domain decision, and writes with `WHERE
version = $locked`. Edits and approval must name the version the client
read (`version_conflict` otherwise). Approval must also name the content
hash the reviewer saw (`content_changed`), and the stored hash is
recomputed to detect tampering. Inside the same transaction approval:

1. re-parses the stored rule through the segment validator;
2. re-runs the content rules for the selected channels;
3. checks every limit with the longest real `first_name` and `city` among
   each selected channel's consented recipients, read in the transaction
   with the consent filter;
4. requires at least one selected channel with a consented recipient;
5. writes status, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, and the audit event.

Re-approving with the same hash is a no-op that returns 200, so retries
and double clicks are safe and record exactly one `approved` event.
Concurrent approvals serialize on the row lock (tested with 8 concurrent
callers against Postgres). Generation and the LLM have no path to approval.

Alternative considered: optimistic locking alone (`UPDATE … WHERE version`
without `FOR UPDATE`). The audience checks must see the campaign that will
be approved, and the lock makes that straightforward. The version
predicate stays as a second guard.

### D34. Edits store invalid content; approval is the gate

PATCH rejects only structurally bad input: unknown channels, invalid UTF-8
or NUL, or text over twice the field's limit. Content that breaks a content
rule (over a limit, unknown variable, HTML, empty) is stored and reported
in `validation`. Rejecting it would discard a marketer's work in progress,
and the preview, which renders stored content, could never show what is
wrong with it. Approval re-validates everything. An edit that changes
nothing does not bump the version, so autosave cannot invalidate a
reviewer's approval for no reason.

### D35. SSE over POST for generation

Draft generation is `POST /campaigns/{id}/drafts` with a
`text/event-stream` response, read with `fetch` and a `ReadableStream`.
`EventSource` only does GET and reconnects automatically. A dropped
connection would then silently start another paid model call.

- Deltas are extracted from the model's partial JSON by a lenient
  incremental scanner (`campaign/stream.go`). It emits only the three
  message fields, keeps escapes and UTF-8 intact across chunk boundaries,
  and never fails. A new `attempt` tells the client to discard the text of
  a rejected attemp
… [truncated 4202 chars]
~~~~

## 🤖 Claude

Backend tests still pass. Adding a regression test for the expired-lease save:

**🔧 Bash** — Add expired-lease regression test with mutation check

~~~~
cat >> internal/campaign/service_test.go <<'EOF'

func TestGeneration_ExpiredLeaseDoesNotSave(t *testing.T) {
	e := newEnv(t)
	c := e.ready(t)
	g, err := e.svc.BeginGeneration(t.Context(), c.ID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	// The lease expires while the generation runs, which lets an edit in.
	e.clock.Advance(time.Minute)
	edited := e.edit(t, c, func(ed *campaign.Edit) { ed.Drafts.SMS.Body = "Edited after expiry" })

	_, err = g.Run(t.Context(), nil)
	requireCode(t, err, campaign.CodeInvalidState)
	got, _ := e.svc.Get(t.Context(), c.ID)
	if got.Drafts.SMS.Body != "Edited after expiry" || got.Version != edited.Version {
		t.Errorf("expired generation overwrote the edit: %+v", got)
	}

## 🤖 Claude

Milestone 4 works end to end except in a real browser, which I didn't open. The UI is covered by component tests, and I ran the whole flow with curl through the frontend's nginx against the Compose stack. A brief turns into an audience with counts, drafts stream in, edits autosave, the preview shows each message personalized for sample recipients with final character counts, and approval is stored and tied to exactly the content that was reviewed. Approval creates no delivery records or messages. Nothing is committed, as you chose.

**Test results**
- `make lint`: golangci-lint 0 issues, ESLint 0 warnings, `vue-tsc` clean.
- `make test`: exit 0. That's 168 Go tests in all packages, Postgres integration tests included, plus 23 frontend tests in 5 files.
- Run against the live stack:
  - **Create:** the example brief gives `audience_ready` with counts 389 / 262 SMS / 246 email.
  - **Stream:** 16 `delta` events over about 0.6 s, then `done`.
  - **Preview:** valid, and the longest seeded name (30 characters) gives an SMS of 103/160.
  - **Rejections:** approving with a stale version or a mismatched hash returns `409`.
  - **Approval:** returns `200`, and re-approving also returns `200` with exactly one `approved` event. Editing afterwards returns `409 invalid_state`.
  - **Tab close:** dropping the connection mid-stream logs the model call as `cancelled`, leaves the campaign unchanged, and a new generation then succeeds.
  - **Logs:** no brief text, message content or contact details in the API logs.

**How it works**
- **Statuses** run `draft → audience_ready → drafts_ready → approved`. Only the backend sets them, and the database enforces the list too.
- **Content hash** is a SHA-256 over the segment rule, selected channels and the three message fields.
- **Writes:** every change locks the campaign row and also checks the version number.
- **Approval** happens in one transaction. It re-checks state, version and hash, and re-validates the rule and content. It also checks every limit against the longest real first name and city among each channel's consented recipients, and needs at least one recipient. There is no code path from the AI to approval.
- **Draft streaming** is Server-Sent Events over a POST, read with `fetch`. A dropped connection therefore never quietly starts another paid model call.
- **One generation per campaign** is enforced by a short lease stored in the database. A second request gets `409`, and the lease expires on its own if a process dies.
- **Audit log:** `campaign_events` is append-only and holds no message content or recipient data.
- **Frontend:** list, new-campaign and review pages using `vue-router` (new dependency). The page aborts the stream when you leave it. Approve is disabled until everything is saved and the current preview passes, and double clicks send one request. No `v-html`.
- **Mock streaming delay:** new setting `LLM_MOCK_STREAM_DELAY` (default 40 ms) slows the mock so streaming is visible.

**Decisions for you to check**
- **Edits save even when content breaks a rule.** An over-limit field or unknown variable is stored and listed in `validation`; only malformed input gets `400`. Otherwise the preview could never show what's wrong. Approval is the gate and rejects it. Recorded as D34 in DECISIONS.md.
- **Failed creation stores nothing.** The campaign is inserted directly as `audience_ready`, so a failed or unsupported brief leaves no dead campaign in the list.
- **Regenerating drafts replaces them, including edits.** The backend allows it; the UI only offers generation before the first drafts exist.

**Fixed after the two-part review** (one reviewer checked the repo's standards, one the milestone spec)
- Message limits now come from the backend instead of being hardcoded in the editor.
- A finished generation no longer saves if its lease expired meanwhile, since an edit could have slipped in. Added a test that fails without this fix.
- One stale doc comment, one wrong comment, and one piece of duplicated error handling.

The spec reviewer found every required test present.

**Known gaps and risks**
- **Cancellation is visible only in the API logs** (`"llm attempt" … "outcome":"cancelled"`). The mock itself logs nothing and has no counter.
- **No authentication.** `approved_by` is free text and edits are attributed to "marketer".
- **Audience counts are a snapshot from creation.** Approval checks the live audience but doesn't refresh them.
- **`internal/campaign/service.go` is about 600 lines.** It could be split.
- **`campaign_events` is not fully locked down.** The trigger blocks updates, but deletes are only documented, not enforced.

**Something I didn't do:** `.claude/skills/implement/SKILL.md` changed during the session (its "Commit your work" line was removed). I didn't edit it, so I left it as it is.

README, DECISIONS.md (D32–D38), CLAUDE.md layout, `.env.example` and `docker-compose.yml` are updated. No `.env` or temporary files are in the working tree.

**Main files**
- Backend: `migrations/000003_campaigns.*`, `internal/campaign/` (`campaign.go`, `service.go`, `stream.go`, `campaigntest/`), `internal/content/channels.go`, `internal/segment/describe.go`, `internal/platform/postgres/campaigns.go`, and `internal/httpapi/` (`campaigns.go`, `drafts_stream.go`). `internal/httpapi/ai.go` and its test are removed.
- Frontend: `src/api/`, `src/views/`, `src/components/`, `src/composables/`, `src/router.ts`.

Suggested commit message:
```
feat(campaign): persisted campaigns with streamed drafts, editing, and human approval

- campaigns + append-only campaign_events (migration 000003)
- domain state machine, content hash, row-locked optimistic updates
- approval re-validates rule/content and checks limits against the real audience
- SSE-over-POST draft generation with DB generation lease and cancellation
- Vue router UI: list, new campaign, review (stream, autosave, preview, approve)
- replaces the temporary /api/v1/ai/* endpoints

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

{"content":"\"\"\"Split a Go file into several files of the same package by moving whole\ntop-level declarations (with their doc comments) verbatim.\n\nusage: gosplit.py SRC MAPPING_JSON\nMAPPING_JSON: {\"files\": {\"edit.go\": [\"Edit\", \"Service.Edit\", ...], ...},\n               \"default\": \"service.go\" | null}\nNames: \"Type\", \"func\", \"Recv.method\", and for var/const blocks the first\nidentifier declared. Every declaration must be mapped unless a default is\ngiven. Each output file gets the source's full import block; run goimports\nafterwards to prune it.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom collections import Counter\n\nsrc_path, mapping_path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]\nmapping = json.load(open(mapping_path))\nsrc = open(src_path).read()\nlines = src.split(\"\\n\")\n\n# Header: everything up to and including the import block.\npkg_line = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if l.startswith(\"package \"))\nimp_start = next(i for i, l in enumerate(lines) if l.startswith(\"import\"))\nif lines[imp_start].rstrip().endswith(\"(\"):\n    imp_end = next(i for i in range(imp_start, len(lines)) if lines[i] == \")\")\nelse:\n    imp_end = imp_start\nheader_pkg = lines[pkg_line]\nheader_imports = \"\\n\".join(lines[imp_start:imp_end + 1])\nif any(l.strip() for l in lines[:pkg_line] if not l.startswith(\"//\")):\n    sys.exit(\"unexpected content before package clause\")\nif pkg_line != 0:\n    sys.exit(\"file comment before package clause not supported\")\n\ndecl_re = re.compile(r\"^(func|type|var|const)\\b\")\nstarts = []\nfor i in range(imp_end + 1, len(lines)):\n    if decl_re.match(lines[i]):\n        j = i\n        while j - 1 > imp_end and lines[j - 1].startswith(\"//\"):\n            j -= 1\n        starts.append((j, i))\n\n\ndef name_of(decl_idx):\n    l = lines[decl_idx]\n    m = re.match(r\"^func \\(\\w+ \\*?(\\w+)\\) (\\w+)\", l)\n    if m:\n        return f\"{m.group(1)}.{m.group(2)}\"\n    m = re.match(r\"^func (\\w+)\", l)\n

## 🤖 Claude

I split `internal/campaign/service.go` by concern, and the preview structs (`Preview`, `ChannelPreview`, `SamplePreview`, `RenderedField`) now live in `preview.go`. Nothing else changed: no renames, no behavior changes, and every exported name is the same. Everything passes, and nothing is committed.

| File | Contents | Lines |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ports.go` | `Store`, `UpdateFunc`, `Write`, `ErrNotFound`/`ErrConflict`, `AudienceInspector`, `ChannelAudience`, `Recipient`, `AIGenerator` | 74 |
| `service.go` | `ServiceConfig`, `ListLimit`, `Service`, `NewService`, `Create`, `Get`, `List`, `storeError`, `update` | 123 (was 593) |
| `edit.go` | `editCaps`, `Edit`, `Service.Edit`, `checkEdit` | 84 |
| `generation.go` | `leaseSlack`/`cleanupTimeout`, `Generation`, `BeginGeneration`, `Run`, `release`, `newToken` | 138 |
| `preview.go` | `Preview`, `ChannelPreview`, `SamplePreview`, `RenderedField`, `Service.Preview`, `inspect`, `hasRecipients`, `render` | 116 |
| `approve.go` | `MaxApproverRunes`, `Approval`, `Approve`, `revalidateRule`, `checkApprover` | 107 |

The tests follow the same split: `edit_test.go`, `generation_test.go`, `preview_test.go` and `approve_test.go`. Shared fixtures and helpers stay in `service_test.go`.

**Skills:** `golang-how-to` pointed to `golang-refactoring` as the main skill for restructuring existing code. I followed its rules: a plan approved by you before editing, a high-coverage starting point (about 90%), and moving code mechanically rather than retyping it.

**How I made sure only file boundaries moved:**
- `gopls` isn't installed, so a small script copied each declaration with its doc comment byte-for-byte. It refused any declaration I hadn't explicitly assigned to a file and checked that none were lost or duplicated. goimports then trimmed each file's imports.
- The lines of the new files, sorted and ignoring imports and blank lines, match the original exactly, both for the code and for the tests.
- `go test -list` shows the same 52 tests before and after.
- The full Go test run with `-race`, the Postgres integration tests and golangci-lint all pass, with 0 lint issues.

The split ends up in the same uncommitted change set as Milestone 4, because those files were all new anyway. If you want it as its own commit, a message like this fits:

```
refactor(campaign): split service.go by concern

Pure file moves within package campaign; no identifier or behavior changes.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

~~~~
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# SessionEnd hook: exports the session transcript to ai-sessions/ as Markdown.
# Re-exporting the same session (e.g. after --resume) overwrites its file.
set -u

if ! command -v jq >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "export-session: jq is not installed; session was not exported." >&2
  exit 0
fi

INPUT="$(cat)"

TRANSCRIPT="$(printf '%s' "$INPUT" | jq -r '.transcript_path // ""')"
SESSION_ID="$(printf '%s' "$INPUT" | jq -r '.session_id // "unknown"')"
REASON="$(printf '%s' "$INPUT" | jq -r '.reason // "unknown"')"
PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$(printf '%s' "$INPUT" | jq -r '.cwd // "."')}"
HOOK_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
OUT_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/ai-sessions"

[[ -f "$TRANSCRIPT" ]] || exit 0

BODY="$(jq -Rr -f "$HOOK_DIR/transcript-to-md.jq" "$TRANSCRIPT")"

# Skip sessions without any conversation (opened and closed immediately).
[[ "$BODY" == *"## 👤 User"* ]] || exit 0

mkdir -p "$OUT_DIR"

SHORT_ID="${SESSION_ID:0:8}"
OUT="$(find "$OUT_DIR" -maxdepth 1 -name "*_${SHORT_ID}.md" -print -quit)"
STARTED="$(jq -Rr 'fromjson? | .timestamp // empty' "$TRANSCRIPT" | head -n 1)"
ENDED="$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
if [[ -z "$OUT" ]]; then
  STAMP="$(date -d "${STARTED:-now}" +%Y-%m-%d_%H%M 2>/dev/null || date +%Y-%m-%d_%H%M)"
  OUT="$OUT_DIR/${STAMP}_${SHORT_ID}.md"
fi

TMP="$(mktemp "$OUT_DIR/.export.XXXXXX")"
{
  echo "# Claude Code Session ${SHORT_ID}"
  echo
  echo "- Session ID: \`${SESSION_ID}\`"
  echo "- Started: ${STARTED:-unknown}"
  echo "- Ended: ${ENDED} (${REASON})"
  echo "- Exported automatically by \`.claude/hooks/export-session.sh\`"
  echo
  echo "---"
  echo
  printf '%s\n' "$BODY"
} | sed -E \
    -e 's/sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}/[REDACTED_ANTHROPIC_KEY]/g' \
    -e 's/(ANTHROPIC_API_KEY[[:space:]]*[=:][[:space:]]*)[^[:space:]"'"'"'`]+/\1[REDACTED]/g' \
    -e 's/(Bearer[[:space:]]+)[A-Za-z0-9._~+\/=-]{16,}/\1[REDACTED]/g' \
  > "$TMP" && chmod 644 "$TMP" && mv "$TMP" "$OUT"

exit 0
42-      }

## 🤖 Claude

I won't edit `.env`. CLAUDE.md says "Never read, print, or edit `.env`", and a project hook blocks it too, so you'll need to make this change yourself.

There's only one new setting from this milestone, and it's optional:

```
# Pause between the mock LLM's streamed draft chunks (max 1s), so the UI
# shows drafts arriving. Ignored with the real model.
LLM_MOCK_STREAM_DELAY=40ms
```

If you leave it out, `docker-compose.yml` falls back to `40ms`. Add it only if you want a different value, for example `100ms` for slower streaming.

To append it without opening the file, type this in the prompt:

```
! printf '\n# Pause between the mock LLM streamed chunks (max 1s)\nLLM_MOCK_STREAM_DELAY=40ms\n' >> .env
```

Then restart the API so it picks the value up: `! docker compose up -d api`.
